# AppRafter — Plan change history

> Plan-level change ledger: one row per (sub-)subphase or patch release. Extracted from
> `plan.md` (formerly its in-file "Plan change history" table) so the roadmap document stays
> forward-looking. For per-release Keep-a-Changelog detail see [`UNRELEASED.md`](./UNRELEASED.md);
> the actionable roadmap and phase ledger live in `plan.md`. Rows preserve source order
> (broadly oldest-first).

| 2026-05-14 | M1.5 Track A.9 — `apprafter bootstrap-all` orchestrator (Phase 1 apply → Phase 2 kubeconfig SSH poll up to 300s × 10s interval → Phase 3 cluster-bootstrap) under a single `indicatif::MultiProgress` UX; `--dry-run` prints the plan without provider/cluster mutation; `--target <name>` is propagated to all phases; `commands::kubeconfig::fetch_and_cache` extracted from `run` so the Phase 2 retry-loop does not spawn child processes; `commands::kubeconfig` now uses `resolve_hetzner_token` instead of reading the env directly; `--target` flag added to `Commands::Kubeconfig`; +8 tests (4 unit + 4 integration); v0.1.84 | initial |
| 2026-05-14 | M1.5 Track A.9 hotfix UX — after the manual walk of v0.1.84: `MultiProgress` duplicated spinner lines on every helm/kubectl `println` (the Phase 1 + Phase 3 spinner fought with the tracing logs of apply/cluster-bootstrap over the same row); dry-run printed a `<active target>` placeholder instead of the active target's name and did not explain what each phase does. v0.1.85 rebuilt bootstrap_all: Phase 1+3 without a spinner (`→ start / inner output / ✓ end`), Phase 2 keeps the spinner (the retry loop owns all output), `finish_and_clear()` + static success line; dry-run now loads `default_config_root` + `resolve_active_target_name` + `load_active_target_config` and prints the real active target name + Provider/Region/Tier/Cluster/SSH-key from `config.yaml` + a human-readable description of each phase; +2 integration tests (empty store hint + active target resolved name); v0.1.85 | initial |
| 2026-05-14 | M1.5 Track A.10 — miette diagnostic refinement: `CliError` derives `miette::Diagnostic` with a stable `code(apprafter::*)` + multi-line `help(...)` on 9 user-facing variants (cue_not_found, hetzner_api_error, server_type_unavailable, state::corrupt, target::invalid_config, target::not_found, io/json/yaml, cli::other); the binary entry point switched from `color-eyre` to `miette::set_hook` with a `fancy` reporter; `NO_COLOR` is respected; the `color-eyre` workspace + platform-cli deps were removed; +11 tests (8 unit on the `.code()`/`.help()` accessor surface + 3 subprocess-based integration tests on rendered stderr — the `help:` block, diagnostic code substrings, ANSI-free under `NO_COLOR`); v0.1.86 | initial |
| 2026-05-14 | M1.5 Track A.10 walk-fix — `target add` with a broken token exited under the generic `apprafter::cli::other` because `commands/target.rs::ping_provider` and `commands/target_wizard.rs::prompt_token` wrapped the typed `CliError::Hetzner { status: 401, .. }` in `CliError::Other(format!(...))`, losing the diagnostic code and rotation help. v0.1.87 added two typed variants with a `#[diagnostic_source]` cause chain: `apprafter::target::token_rejected` (401 — rotation hint + console URL + clipboard newline trap) and `apprafter::target::provider_unreachable` (non-401 / transport — `apprafter doctor` + status page + `--no-ping`); a shared `classify_ping_error(provider, err)` helper at both call sites; miette now renders a two-layer cascade — an outer summary with rotation help + a chained inner `hetzner_api_error` with a full 401/403/429/5xx breakdown; +2 unit tests on the new variants (`provider_token_rejected_carries_rotation_hint_and_chains_cause` proves that `diagnostic_source()` reaches the inner code, `provider_api_unreachable_targets_outage_path_not_rotation` guarantees that an outage does not suggest rotation); 2 prior target_test integration tests migrated their assertions from the wrapper to diagnostic codes (rendered output line-wraps, substrings such as `(status 401)` are unreliable, codes are reliable); v0.1.87 | initial |
| 2026-05-14 | M1.5 Track A.11 — semantic colors + subcommand aliases: a new `cli_core::style` module on top of `owo-colors` (auto-honours `NO_COLOR` via the `supports-colors` feature) with 6 helpers (ok/warn/fail/info/dim/bold); color applied to `bootstrap-all` phase markers (`→` cyan, `✓` green, `✗` red) + `doctor` glyphs (green ✓ / yellow ⚠ / red ✗); 6 new subcommand aliases (`kubeconfig` ↔ `kc`, `cluster-bootstrap` ↔ `cb`, `bootstrap-all` ↔ `up`, `target list/show/remove` ↔ `ls`/`info`/`rm`), preserving the prior `target` ↔ `t` for chained `apprafter t ls`; +2 unit (style ANSI-strip under non-TTY) + 7 integration (alias subprocess routing including chained `t ls`); v0.1.88 | initial |
| 2026-05-14 | M1.5 Track A.11 walk-fix — after the walk of v0.1.88: `apprafter up --dry-run` remained monochrome (I had only colored the wet path); only the `INFO` label from the tracing-subscriber was glowing green. v0.1.89 covered the dry-run plan: `bootstrap-all` heading + `Target:` label + active target name via `style::bold`; `(active)`/`(via --target override)` tag via `style::info` cyan; `[1/3]`/`[2/3]`/`[3/3]` phase numbers cyan (echoing the wet path's `→`); `Provider:/Region:/Tier:/...` labels + `<unset — ...>` placeholders + bottom hint via `style::dim` so that defaults do not distract from the real values; script(1)-replay confirms ANSI bytes are present in a TTY and absent under `NO_COLOR=1`; v0.1.89 | initial |

| 2026-05-15 | M1.5 Track A.9c (Phase 2 polish backlog closure): `SshKubeconfigFetcher::build_command` adds `-o ConnectTimeout=5` so the first kubeconfig-poll attempt fails in 5s instead of the kernel-default ~30s while cloud-init brings up sshd on the new cpx22; the Phase 2 label rename `[2/3] kubeconfig` → `[2/3] k3s-ready` (the old label was misleading — ~60s is cloud-init+k3s startup, not the fetch itself) applied consistently: spinner, success/failure markers, dry-run plan, total breakdown summary; the spinner message is now "waiting for cloud-init + k3s on the new node…"; docs (quickstart, troubleshooting, cli reference) updated synchronously; +1 regression test (`ssh_fetcher_caps_connect_timeout_at_five_seconds`); the existing `bootstrap_all_dry_run_prints_three_phase_plan_without_provider_calls` integration test updated for `[2/3] k3s-ready`; a typical Phase 2 on a Hetzner cpx22 + Ubuntu 24.04 is expected to drop from ~60s to ~20-40s; v0.1.91 — closes the entire Track A backlog | initial |
| 2026-05-15 | M1.5 Track B.1.66 — platform-stack scaffold: a new top-level `platform-stack/cue/` (flat layout — CUE treats subdirs as separate package instances even when the `package` declaration matches, which broke cross-file `_components` merging; discovered during the design walk via `cue export -e direct_cilium`). 12 CUE files: `platform.cue` (umbrella schema — `#Version`/`#Channel`/`#Tier`/`#ComponentSource`/`#Component`/`#ComponentSet`/`#PlatformValues`/`_components`), 8 component declarations (`component_cilium.cue`, `component_cert-manager.cue`, `component_argocd.cue`, `component_apprafter-operator.cue`, `component_admission-webhook.cue`, `component_backstage.cue`, `component_network-policies.cue`, `component_argocd-cue-cmp.cue` — the last declared but disabled by default until the wiring step in 1.69), 2 tier overlays (`tier_solo.cue` tier 1 + `tier_team.cue` tier 2 with their own `enabled`/replicas/Hubble settings), `compatibility.cue` with a `#ChangeClass`+`#VersionRecord` schema and an initial 0.2.0 entry; the design walk surfaced a gotcha: the `[NAME=string]: #Component & { name: NAME }` autobinding in `#ComponentSet` re-applies `#Component` on each overlay-unification and strips the concrete `namespace`/`version` — replaced with a plain `[string]: #Component` + an explicit `name:` per component; `_components: #ComponentSet` also cannot be typed (the same problem), left as a plain `{}` with local type-conformance at the declaration site; a `Chart.yaml.tmpl` template + `README.md` (full layout + contribution model + distribution + forking + design-walk decision rationale) + `CHANGELOG.md` (0.2.0 planned entry); `scripts/lint-cue.sh` + `scripts/check-spdx-headers.sh` extended for `platform-stack/cue/...` + `platform-stack/Chart.yaml.tmpl`; `cue vet -c` passes, lint clean, all 565 Rust tests remain green; v0.1.92 — opens Track B | initial |
| 2026-05-15 | M1.5 Track B.1.67 — `cue cmd render` pipeline + umbrella chart generation: `platform-stack/cue/render_tool.cue` declares `command: render: { ... }` with 9 tasks (3 `file.Mkdir` + 6 `file.Create`) and a `$dep` chain for DAG ordering; emits Chart.yaml v2 (with the annotations apprafter.io/change-class+apprafter.io/operator-version from compatibility.cue), values.yaml (defaults to tier1), values.schema.json (hand-rolled Helm-native draft-2020-12 — CUE's auto-export targets draft-07 which Helm does not understand), templates/applications.yaml (a single Go template iterating over .Values.components → one Argo CD Application per enabled entry, conditional helm.valuesObject only when source.chart is set, labels apprafter.io/{component,tier,channel}), compatibility.yaml, examples/values.{solo,team}.yaml, README.md inside the rendered chart; `platform-stack/Makefile` with targets `render`/`render-only`/`lint`/`clean`/`help`, auto-detect cue+helm with nix fallback, version resolved from tier1.version via `cue export` without hardcoding; the `Justfile` gained `platform-stack-render` + `platform-stack-check` wrappers; `dist/` is already gitignored project-wide; README local-dev section updated with the real commands + a schema-gate sanity check; verified — `helm lint` clean (a single INFO about the chart icon), `helm template` default → 6 tier-1 Apps, with team values → 7 Apps (Backstage on), `--set tier=99` → schema rejects "value must be one of 1, 2, 3, 4"; v0.1.93 | initial |
| 2026-05-15 | M1.5 Track B.1.68 — CI OCI publish workflow + cosign signing: `.github/workflows/platform-stack-publish.yml` triggers on a `platform-stack/v*` tag (+ `workflow_dispatch` with a `version:` input); validates `compatibility.cue` via `scripts/check-platform-stack-version.sh` (cue export -e compatibility[<v>], exits 1 with a pointer-to-fix if the entry is missing); renders the chart (`make -C platform-stack render-only`); `helm lint` + tier-1/tier-2 smoke template assertions (6 / 7 enabled Apps); `helm package` → `.tgz`; `docker login` to ghcr.io via GITHUB_TOKEN; `helm push` to `oci://ghcr.io/<owner>` (Helm 3.8+ native OCI); resolve immutable digest via `helm show chart --version`; cosign keyless sign by digest (Sigstore OIDC + `id-token: write` permission, no managed keys); cosign sign-blob on the `.tgz` → `.tgz.sig` + `.tgz.pem` for the GitHub Release attachment path; `oras tag :latest` on stable releases with a graceful warning if the CLI is absent; `gh release create` with heredoc-built notes via mktemp+`--notes-file` (install snippets Argo CD + plain Helm + cosign verify for both) + `.tgz` / `.sig` / `.pem` attachments + `--prerelease` flag on a pre-release; security hardening — all dynamic inputs (github.ref_name, github.repository_owner, github.event.inputs.version, github.repository) routed through env: binding (the same anti-injection pattern as release-operator.yml); `platform-stack/RELEASE.md` — full maintainer procedure (semver rules, pre-release checklist, tagging, after-publish actions, failure-mode recovery); local validation: yamllint clean, scripts/check-platform-stack-version.sh tested on the happy (0.2.0 → YAML) + unhappy (99.99.99 → exit 1) paths; final acceptance ⏳ verifies after the first real `platform-stack/v0.2.0-rc1` push (CI-side, not locally reproducible); v0.1.94 | initial |
| 2026-05-15 | M1.5 Track B.1.68 walk-fix — GitHub Actions rejected the workflow on push with the error "Line: 232, Col: 14: Unexpected symbol: '…'" because one of the comments inside a `run: |` block contained `${{ … }}` (a literal Unicode ellipsis inside expression-syntax braces) — GHA parses `${{ }}` in `run`-body scalars before the shell sees the script. v0.1.95 rewrote that comment without `${{ }}` syntax, verified with grep that there are no other expression-style braces in `run` bodies (header comments outside scalars are discarded by yamllint — line 29 there is harmless); yamllint clean, the remaining gates green; the workflow is now syntactically valid for the GitHub Actions parser; v0.1.95 | initial |
| 2026-05-15 | M1.5 chart-versioning policy decision — the first published platform-stack version = **0.1.0** instead of the previously planned 0.2.0. Rationale: the chart MINOR tracks the phase number of the AppRafter monorepo (Phase 1.5 → chart 0.1.x), not the milestone target. When Phase 2 services land → the chart MINOR will bump to 0.2.0 alongside `v0.2.0-services`. Patch versions of the chart and the monorepo are independent (they share only MINOR/MAJOR semantics). v0.1.96 flipped: `tier_solo.cue` + `tier_team.cue` `version: "0.1.0"`, `compatibility.cue` entry rename, `platform.cue` doc-comment, 4 component doc-comments (cilium/cert-manager/argocd-cue-cmp/operator/webhook mentioning "platform-stack X.Y.Z"), `CHANGELOG.md` section + version notes, `RELEASE.md` versioning rules + tagging examples; re-rendering produces `dist/platform-stack-0.1.0/`, helm lint clean, `check-platform-stack-version.sh 0.1.0` → success, `0.2.0` → exit 1; v0.1.96 | initial |
| 2026-05-19 | M1.5 Track B.1.68 refactor — chart-version single-source-of-truth + the workflow inverts the tag↔publish ordering. Walk-found: tier_solo/tier_team/compatibility-key all hardcoded "0.1.0" as a literal → potential drift. The chart's `component_cilium.cue` remains the owner of upgrades via Argo CD adoption (same release name + namespace, `prune: false`). The `KubectlRunner::wait_for_condition` API changed: `namespace: &str → namespace: Option<&str>` (cluster-scoped resources must not carry `-n`). 3 new tests (`wait_command_emits_namespace_flag_when_some`, `wait_command_omits_namespace_flag_when_none`, `cilium_installs_before_argocd_so_node_can_become_ready` — a regression guard on the ordering); the existing main test renamed for the new sequence; FakeKubectl in `cluster_bootstrap` + `argocd_password` updated for `Option<&str>`. 554 passed (was 551). | 2026-05-19 | M1.5 Track B.1.70 walk-fix #3 — the third real-Hetzner walk of v0.1.99 surfaced **two coupled bugs** in the GitOps loader path: (1) Argo CD does not infer the OCI Helm protocol from the `oci://` scheme — it shells out to `helm pull --repo oci://... <chart>` which `helm` rejects with `object required`; OCI registries require `helm pull oci://<repo>/<chart>` (chart-name inline), and Argo CD generates the correct form ONLY when the repo is registered via `Secret(label=repository, enableOCI: "true")`. (2) `kubectl wait --for=jsonpath={.status.health.status}=Healthy` on the root Application — a false positive: a freshly-created Application with zero rendered children is trivially Healthy (no resources to fail), even when Sync=Unknown (the chart pull errored). The v0.1.99 loader matched empty-Healthy and returned successfully, leaving the cluster without child Applications. v0.1.100 fix: (a) `APPRAFTER_PLATFORM_STACK_DEFAULT_REPO` "oci://ghcr.io/apprafter" → "ghcr.io/apprafter" (bare URL), (b) `argocd_loader_values_yaml` gains a `configs.repositories.apprafter` block (url + type: helm + enableOCI: "true"), (c) the chart's `component_argocd.cue` mirrors the same block in values so that self-reconcile preserves the registration on adoption, (d) the chart's `component_apprafter-operator.cue` + `component_admission-webhook.cue` dropped the `oci://` prefix in repoURL, (e) `perform_bootstrap` step 4 split into 4a (wait Synced) + 4b (wait Healthy) — Synced is the correct first signal that the chart pulled and children rendered. 3 new tests (`argocd_loader_values_register_apprafter_oci_repo` with a per-line oci:// negative guard, `root_application_repourl_is_bare_without_oci_scheme`), the main bootstrap test extended for 4 waits. 556 passed (was 554). Chart bumped 0.1.3 → 0.1.4 with a compat entry + a known-issue note in the 0.1.3 notes; CLI bumped 0.1.99 → 0.1.100, `RELEASED_PLATFORM_STACK_VERSION` → "0.1.4". Manual diagnosis on the live cluster: creating `Secret(repo-apprafter)` + patching the root Application's repoURL → 6 child Applications appeared instantly (cert-manager Synced/Healthy first; the rest Sync=Unknown initially — the controller catches up) | v0.1.100 |

| 2026-05-24 | M1.5 walk-fix #10 post-B.1.79a — CMP discover stdout fix (cue-cmp v0.1.4 → v0.1.5, chart 0.1.45 → 0.1.46). After walk-fix #8 (command-based discover) + walk-fix #8b (cue-cmp v0.1.4 image rebuild), landing apps STILL failed with the same `Failed to unmarshal "package.json": Object 'Kind' is missing` (cached). After `kubectl exec deployment/argocd-redis -- redis-cli FLUSHDB` + the `argocd.argoproj.io/refresh=hard` annotation on each Application, the error reproduced identically — not a stale-cache artefact, the CMP plugin genuinely did not match the repo. Diagnosis: `kubectl -n argocd logs deployment/argocd-repo-server -c cue-cmp` showed the discover shell snippet running, exiting 0, but each invocation was followed by `Plugin command returned zero output`. Root cause: Argo CD's `MatchRepository` in `cmpserver/plugin/plugin.go` keys on the **stdout** of the discover command, not the exit code; the v0.1.4 snippet piped `find -print -quit` through `grep -q .`, and `-q` (quiet) suppresses stdout entirely. Even when find matched the real `landing/web/apprafter/Application.cue`, no output reached runCommand's return value, the plugin was marked `IsSupported: false`, and the directory-mode fallback re-fired the `package.json` parse exactly as before walk-fix #8. Fix: drop the `\| grep -q .` pipe from BOTH branches of the discover snippet in the `argocd-cue-cmp/plugin.yaml` source-of-truth and the chart's `extraObjects` ConfigMap mirror in `platform-stack/cue/component_argocd.cue`. `find -print -quit` itself prints the first matched path on hit, prints nothing on miss; both code paths exit 0. Stdout emptiness IS the signal Argo CD reads. New `argocd-cue-cmp/test-discover.sh` regression test: extracts the discover shell snippet from plugin.yaml directly (no duplication — drift would bite), runs it against 5 fixture directories (parent-dir convention `apprafter/Application.cue` subdir, cwd-is-apprafter depth-1, filename-prefix `apprafter*.cue` at root, plain repo without apprafter files must return empty stdout, generic `.cue` without apprafter prefix must not match) — asserts that stdout non-emptiness matches the expected match/no-match signal, NOT just the exit code. Plus a hard string-match guard `grep -Fq 'grep -q'` fails the test immediately if reintroduced. Wired into `.github/workflows/argocd-cue-cmp-check.yml` between the image build and drift detection. Memory `feedback_cmp_discover_test.md` saved with the rule: every change to plugin.yaml must run test-discover.sh; Argo CD reads stdout not the exit code; specific traps to watch (`grep -q`, `>/dev/null`, `cmp /dev/null -`, etc.). No CLI bump per the v0.1.159 policy. cue-cmp v0.1.4 → v0.1.5 (image rebuild with the baked plugin.yaml), chart 0.1.45 → 0.1.46 (`_components.argocd-cue-cmp.values.image.tag` follows via CUE import + ConfigMap content updated). Compat entry `compatibility: "0.1.46"` shipped | argocd-cue-cmp v0.1.5 + platform-stack 0.1.46 |
| 2026-05-24 | M1.5 walk-fix #9 post-B.1.79a — the CLI resolves the latest platform-stack version from the upstream GitHub Releases API at bootstrap time. The operator asked why the CLI had to bump on every chart-only release; investigation surfaced that `cluster_bootstrap::platform_stack_version()` returned the baked-in `RELEASED_PLATFORM_STACK_VERSION` constant verbatim. The constant tracks `platform.cue`'s `currentVersion` via `cli-providers/build.rs` at compile time. This coupling forced a CLI rebuild + release on every chart bump just to keep fresh installs current — wrong by design (the CLI binary has no business knowing the latest chart version at compile time; the latest chart is whatever is published to the upstream release stream). Fix: a new `cli_providers::k8s::channel_latest` module with a `resolve_latest_platform_stack_version()` entry point. It GETs `https://api.github.com/repos/apprafter/apprafter/releases?per_page=100` with a 3s timeout, filters `tag_name` to entries starting with `platform-stack/v`, strips the prefix, parses as semver, picks the highest with stable preferred over prerelease. Any failure path (network / parse / no matching tags) falls back to the baked constant so air-gapped / firewalled installs still bootstrap. `cluster_bootstrap::platform_stack_version()` now calls the resolver. Stable / prerelease semantics: when both stable and prerelease are published, picks stable; only when ALL candidates are prereleases (fresh channel before the first stable cut) returns one. Uses the GitHub Releases API (the same surface as `version_check.rs`) rather than the OCI registry tag list — no auth token needed, same rate-limit story (the resolver fires only at bootstrap, rare). +7 unit tests on the pure `pick_latest_platform_stack_tag(releases)` helper covering the mixed-streams happy path, the no-platform-stack-tags fork case, malformed entries skipped, non-array defensive, stable vs prerelease precedence, prerelease fallback when no stable, two-digit-patch semver vs lexical regression guard. **Versioning policy update:** going forward chart-only bumps do NOT bump the CLI — the v0.1.158 bump that motivated this question was the last of its kind. Future chart releases are picked up by existing CLI binaries through the new resolver, no CLI rebuild needed. CLI 0.1.158 → 0.1.159. Chart unchanged. cue-cmp unchanged | v0.1.159 |

The walk-fix #8 reasoning that "the ConfigMap mount overrides the image's plugin.yaml at runtime" is correct for chart-installed clusters, but operators who install cue-cmp manually (attaching to a stock Argo CD without the AppRafter chart) get the image's baked-in plugin.yaml — stale image content = wrong behaviour for that path. The drift policy is enforced rightly. | 2026-05-24 | M1.5 walk-fix #8 post-B.1.79a — CMP discover switches from glob to command (chart 0.1.44) + the wizard strips the trailing `apprafter` segment. An operator on chart 0.1.43 registered landing apps via `apprafter app add --path landing/cms`; the Argo CD UI surfaced `Failed to unmarshal "package.json": Object 'Kind' is missing` for both `landing-web` and `landing-cms`. Diagnostics confirmed the chart at 0.1.43, the configmap with the new brace-alternation glob, the `MatchRepository` gRPC calls returning OK (success) but the absence of any downstream `generate` activity implied all match calls returned false. Root cause: chart 0.1.42-0.1.43 plugin.yaml used `discover.find.glob: "{**/apprafter*.cue,**/apprafter/**/*.cue}"` — brace alternation that `doublestar/v4` supports, but Argo CD 2.13.1's vendored doublestar either treats the braces literally or never received v4 brace support. CMP returned no-match; Argo CD fell back to the default directory mode, walked `landing/cms/`, and tried to parse `package.json` as a k8s manifest. Fix: switch `discover.find.glob` to `discover.find.command` (a find-based shell snippet running from the path's directory, exits 0 on match) — handles both conventions in one expression: cwd basename `apprafter` → match any `.cue` at depth 1, otherwise → match any `.cue` inside the `apprafter/` subdir OR with a filename starting `apprafter`. `-print -quit | grep -q .` short-circuits after the first match. No doublestar dependency. The cue-cmp Docker image is unchanged at v0.1.3 — the entire fix lives in the chart-managed `cue-cmp-plugin-config` ConfigMap content. A chart upgrade + argocd-repo-server rollout-restart picks up the new discovery rule. Wizard polish bundled: a new pure `strip_apprafter_tail(rel)` helper strips the trailing `apprafter` segment so `cd landing/cms/apprafter && apprafter app add` defaults to `landing/cms` (the parent-of-apprafter form is the cleaner convention; CMP handles either with the new command-based discover, but the wizard suggests the canonical shape). +5 unit tests on `strip_apprafter_tail`. Operator recovery: `apprafter platform upgrade --to 0.1.44` + `kubectl rollout restart deployment/argocd-repo-server -n argocd` + a hard-refresh of affected apps. CLI 0.1.156 → 0.1.157. | 2026-05-24 | M1.5 walk-fix #7 post-B.1.79a — migration helper observability + cross-mount safe rename + richer-legacy preference. Two operator-impacting bugs surfaced on the v0.1.155 walk: (a) the operator ran `apprafter app add` from `cli/`, hit `no cached kubeconfig in state` — inspection found `cli/.apprafter/state.json` (5375 bytes, a real hetzner_cloud + kubeconfig) AND `~/.config/apprafter/state/dev/.apprafter/state.json` (412 bytes, a placeholder with round-number IDs server_id=42 firewall_id=200 floating_ip_ids=[300], kubeconfig null). The v0.1.154-155 migration helper's silent "new-wins by existence" branch preserved the placeholder and left the rich legacy file unmigrated, with no stderr signal — the operator had no idea why their real state was not loading. (b) After manually deleting the placeholder per-target file to force migration, hit `Invalid cross-device link (os error 18)`. The initial "different physical partitions" framing was inaccurate: the operator's `/projects/` and `$HOME` were on the SAME btrfs filesystem, just different subvolumes mounted at separate paths (a common NixOS layout). EXDEV fires on ANY mount-point boundary crossing: btrfs subvolumes, ZFS datasets in one pool, bind mounts, overlay/fuse mounts — all return EXDEV on rename across the boundary regardless of whether the bytes physically share a block device. `std::fs::rename` is a single-syscall with no fallback. Three coordinated fixes: (1) richer-legacy preference — when both files exist, parse both and compare hetzner_cloud presence via a new pure `read_has_hetzner_cloud(path)`; if the legacy is richer than the per-target (legacy has hetzner_cloud, target does not), back the placeholder up to `state.json.bak` and migrate the legacy as authoritative. (2) A loud stderr notice on the genuine skip path (both files carry hetzner_cloud — two real clusters case); silent skip removed entirely. (3) A new `cross_fs_safe_rename(src, dst)` helper tries `fs::rename` first, falls back to `fs::copy` + `fs::remove_file` on EXDEV (os error 18 on Linux + macOS); all three rename callsites in the migration helper (state move, known_hosts move, backup rename) routed through it. +2 unit tests: `migrate_legacy_prefers_richer_legacy_when_per_target_lacks_hetzner_cloud` (the exact v0.1.155 walk scenario), `migrate_legacy_keeps_per_target_when_both_carry_hetzner_cloud` (defensive — two real clusters case). Total cli-state migration tests: 5 → 7. CLI 0.1.155 → 0.1.156. Chart unchanged | v0.1.156 |

(c) `argocd-cue-cmp/plugin.yaml` + the chart's embedded plugin.yaml in `component_argocd.cue`'s extraObjects ConfigMap extend the glob to `{**/apprafter*.cue,**/apprafter/**/*.cue}` via doublestar v4 brace alternation — matches both the filename-prefix convention (`apprafter-web.cue`) and the directory-marker convention (`landing/web/apprafter/Application.cue`). | 2026-05-24 | M1.5 walk-fix #4 post-B.1.79a — version_check picks the wrong tag series + 24h → 6h TTL. A user ran the v0.1.142 binary after the v0.1.150 release went live; the banner stayed silent. Cache inspection: `{"latest_tag":"platform-stack/v0.1.38","fetched_at_secs":...}` — a chart-stream tag, not a CLI version. Root cause: the monorepo publishes four release streams (canonical CLI `vX.Y.Z`, chart `platform-stack/vX.Y.Z`, landing `landing-vX.Y.Z`, argocd-cmp `argocd-cue-cmp/vX.Y.Z`), and the `/releases/latest` API endpoint returns the most recently CREATED release across all four. v0.1.149's `version_check` took whatever came back; `trim_start_matches('v')` did not strip the `platform-stack/` prefix; `semver::parse` failed silently; the banner was suppressed. Fix: switch the endpoint to `/releases?per_page=30` (newest-first array). A new pure helper `pick_canonical_cli_tag(releases)` walks the array and returns the first `tag_name` whose stripped form parses as a canonical `semver::Version` — all four prefixed streams fail `Version::parse` naturally without an explicit denylist. The cache also gains defensive validation in `read_fresh_cache`: a non-semver `latest_tag` is treated as a cache miss, clearing leftover garbage from pre-fix caches without operators deleting the file manually. TTL 24h → 6h (the project is in active daily development; the 6h dial keeps operators current without burning GitHub API quota). +8 unit tests on the pure helpers: canonicalise_cli_tag for each of the 4 prefix permutations + empty/garbage + pre-release suffix happy path; pick_canonical_cli_tag for the mixed-stream window happy path + no-CLI-tag-in-window pathological + malformed release-object skip + non-array defensive. Total version_check module: 4 → 13 tests. CLI 0.1.150 → 0.1.151. Chart unchanged | v0.1.151 |

| 2026-05-24 | M1.5 polish post-B.1.79a #5 — the CLI release workflow. Walk feedback on v0.1.135's npm-style newer-release banner: the banner never fires even when the CLI is many tags behind the tip — root cause: `version_check::resolve_latest_tag` polls `https://api.github.com/repos/apprafter/apprafter/releases/latest` but the monorepo had no workflow creating GitHub Releases on `v0.*` tag pushes. The Releases page is empty → the API 404s → the fail-quiet banner stays silent. Fix: a new `.github/workflows/release-cli.yml` triggers on `v0.*` / `v1.*` tag pushes (plus a manual `workflow_dispatch` for re-builds), builds the `apprafter` CLI binary for 3 targets (x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu native on ubuntu-latest, x86_64-apple-darwin cross-compiled on macos-latest, aarch64-apple-darwin native on macos-latest), packages each into `apprafter-<tag>-<target>.tar.gz` (stripped binary + README + LICENSE) with a sha256 sidecar, creates a GitHub Release with the assets uploaded. A verify step runs `apprafter --version` against the fresh binary and asserts the output matches the tag minus the `v` prefix — catches forgotten Cargo.toml bumps before they ship a broken courtesy banner. The release body is autogenerated by pulling the first `##` changelog section that mentions the tag from UNRELEASED.md; falls back to a short default for chart-only / landing-stream tags without dedicated entries. Security: every potentially-tainted input (inputs.tag, github.ref_name, extracted changelog text) flows through an `env:` binding before reaching the shell `run:` — no `${{ }}` interpolation inside the body, eliminating the action-injection class. The tag-glob trigger constrains ref_name to a known-safe shape. The manual re-run path deletes the existing release first to avoid `gh release create` errors on duplicate asset names. Linux aarch64 deliberately absent — tier-1 ships on x86 only; cross-compile via `cross`/QEMU is a separate complication for Phase 2+. CLI 0.1.148 → 0.1.149. Chart unchanged. First release to land on the GitHub Releases page once tagged + pushed → makes the newer-release banner functional for all future tags | v0.1.149 |

Source preference order: PlatformStack.status wins over the Argo CD targetRevision because during a bump cycle the Argo CD Application's targetRevision may briefly lag behind the umbrella's reconciled state — the umbrella patches its children's targetRevision on each component cycle, and mid-flight the value reflects the OLD version until the patch lands; PlatformStack.status, when populated, surfaces the operator's intended version. +4 unit tests on the pure resolver: `_prefers_platformstack_status` (both sources present, status wins), `_falls_back_to_argocd_target_revision` (the M1.5-typical state succeeds via the Argo CD fallback instead of erroring — load-bearing for the operator-facing fix), `_errors_when_both_sources_empty` (clean error message with the --version hint), `_handles_argocd_app_without_target_revision` (defensive: a malformed CR with an empty spec.source treated like a 404). | 2026-05-24 | M1.5 walk-fix #2 post-B.1.79a — AppProjects as standalone umbrella manifests at sync-wave -30. Operator upgrade 0.1.39 → 0.1.40 hit `Unable to refresh admission-webhook: app is not allowed in project "platform", or the project does not exist`. Root cause: chart 0.1.40 (part 1) shipped 4 AppProject definitions via `_loaderValues.argocd.values.configs.projects` — the argocd subchart renders those as AppProject CRs only when it syncs (sync-wave -15). Child Applications at sync-wave 0 (admission-webhook, apprafter-operator, network-policies, backstage, argocd-cue-cmp) reference `spec.project: platform` (the new default from part 1). **Argo CD's app-of-applications model does NOT strictly serialise sync-waves between separate Applications** — only within a single Application's child-resource sync. Across multiple umbrella-managed Applications, Argo CD treats each as an independent reconciler and starts them roughly together. Race: admission-webhook (wave 0) sometimes tries to refresh BEFORE the AppProject `platform` has landed via argocd (wave -15). Fix: ship the AppProject CRs as **standalone manifests in the umbrella chart** at sync-wave -30 (earlier than even Cilium at -20). A new shared `_appProjects` map in `platform-stack/cue/app_projects.cue` carries the 4 definitions; two sites consume it — a new `templates/appprojects.yaml` template (umbrella) emits one `kind: AppProject` per entry at sync-wave -30 on every umbrella sync; `_loaderValues.argocd.values.configs.projects` keeps the same definitions for the initial `apprafter cluster-bootstrap` install before any umbrella sync has run. Both sites describe identical resources (same group/kind/name/namespace); the Argo CD reconciler treats them as one logical object — the duplication is idempotent. A new CUE shape `#AppProjectSpec` constrains entries to the AppProject fields the umbrella actually sets (description, sourceRepos, destinations, cluster/namespace resource whitelists). `#PlatformValues` gains a top-level `appProjects: [string]: #AppProjectSpec`; the tier overlays (tier_solo + tier_team) wire `appProjects: _appProjects`. The fix is structural — fresh installs on 0.1.41+ and 0.1.40 → 0.1.41 upgrades both work without manual intervention. Existing 0.1.40 operators who want immediate refresh-error clearance (instead of waiting for the chart-pull + reconcile cycle) can apply the 4 AppProjects inline via `kubectl apply -f` — an optional unblocker, not a workaround. CUE renders cleanly; `helm template` against the rendered chart emits 4 AppProject CRs at sync-wave -30 alongside the existing child Applications. Chart 0.1.40 → 0.1.41. | 2026-05-24 | M1.5 polish post-B.1.79a #2 — `apprafter platform status` UX (dates / sort / width). Walk feedback: raw RFC3339 timestamps unreadable at a glance ("when did that check happen?"), the `versionHistory` table ordered by JSON document position rather than `appliedAt` desc (brittle across server-side re-sorts), the Conditions table sprawled past 80-col terminals because the old heuristic wrapped MESSAGE to a flat 60 chars without accounting for total layout width. Three coordinated fixes: (1) a new pure `format_timestamp_with_relative(raw, now)` helper parses RFC3339 via `chrono`, renders as `"2026-05-24 14:30 UTC (2 hours ago)"`; the humanise scale uses operator-relevant granularity (under 45s → "just now", under 1h → minutes, under 1d → hours, under 30d → days, under 1y → months, else years); unparseable input surfaces verbatim so audit data is never lost. (2) a new `collect_history_rows(status, now, take)` sorts by parsed `appliedAt` desc using `DateTime::parse_from_rfc3339`; entries with unparseable timestamps sink to the bottom so corrupt / mid-write CRs do not dominate the visible head. (3) a new `render_conditions_table(conditions)` queries `terminal_size::terminal_size()` for the TTY width, computes a budget by subtracting non-MESSAGE column max widths + separator overhead, wraps MESSAGE to the remainder; falls back to 100 columns when stdout is piped. New workspace deps: `chrono` (clock+serde, no default features) and `terminal_size`. The `print_status` signature gains a `now: DateTime<Utc>` parameter so tests can pin a deterministic reference moment for relative-date assertions. +7 unit tests on the pure helpers (format_timestamp_renders_absolute_and_relative, format_timestamp_handles_unparseable_input, humanise_relative_uses_just_now_under_45_seconds, humanise_relative_handles_minutes_hours_days_months_years span coverage, collect_history_rows_sorts_by_applied_at_desc, collect_history_rows_puts_unparseable_timestamps_last, collect_history_rows_caps_at_take). Total platform module: 2 → 9 tests. CLI 0.1.143 → 0.1.144. Chart unchanged | v0.1.144 |

Repo-wide `grep -cP '\p{Cyrillic}'` returns 0 across the entire `cli/` tree. Standardised common phrases: confirmation prompts read `Confirm?`, cancellation prints `Cancelled.`, hints prefixed `Hint:`. Test names and assertion messages translated alongside production code so test bodies match the new English error strings. CLI 0.1.142 → 0.1.143. Chart unchanged | v0.1.143 |

| 2026-05-22 | M1.5 Walk-fix #1 post-B.1.79 — `apprafter open argocd` SIGPIPE early-exit. Walk of v0.1.135: the command printed the credentials banner and immediately exited to the shell prompt without blocking on port-forward. Root cause: `wait_port_forward_ready` took `child.stdout` via `take()`, read lines until `Forwarding from`, and returned — the `BufReader`+`ChildStdout` were dropped, closing the read-end of the pipe. kubectl is a Go binary; Go's default SIGPIPE handler terminates the process on the next write to a closed stdout, and after the initial ready line `kubectl port-forward` emits another `Forwarding from [::1]:…\n` → SIGPIPE → kubectl exit → `child.wait()` returned instantly. Stderr had the same latent class of bugs — `Stdio::piped()` without a drainer; the 64KiB pipe buffer could overflow and block the child on its next write. **Fix:** spawn drainer threads for both pipes. `spawn_ready_drainer` reads stdout line by line, signals readiness via `mpsc::sync_channel::<()>(1)` on the first `Forwarding from` line, then **keeps draining to EOF** — the pipe stays open for the entire lifetime of the child. If EOF arrives before the banner, the sender drops, `recv()` → `Err` → the caller surfaces "exited before binding local port". `spawn_silent_drainer` drains stderr to EOF without surfacing it. +4 unit tests driven by `std::io::Cursor` fakes (without real kubectl): `ready_drainer_signals_on_forwarding_line` (happy path), `ready_drainer_continues_draining_after_signal` (load-bearing — wraps the reader in a Tracker counting consumed bytes, asserts ALL bytes including post-banner ones are read), `ready_drainer_yields_recv_err_when_eof_before_banner` (error surfacing), `silent_drainer_reads_to_eof` (stderr drain coverage). CLI 0.1.135 → 0.1.136. Chart unchanged (CLI-only IO handling defect) | v0.1.136 |

| 2026-05-23 | M1.5 Walk-fix #8 post-B.1.78 — destructive classification was per-target-version, not per-transition. The user pointed out: a cluster on 0.1.35 with walk-platform-1's `platform-0-1-35-to-0-1-36` MigrationPlan in rejected phase. Chart 0.1.37 publishes as safe. autoUpgrade tries to bump 0.1.35 → 0.1.37 — plan name `platform-0-1-35-to-0-1-37` (a different pair from the rejected one) — fetch_change_class returns Safe for 0.1.37's single record → straight bump. **It silently bypasses the operator's reject decision on 0.1.36's breaking content.** Per spec.md §3.11 implied semantics ("any path to the target must respect the strictest class encountered"), classification should be path-aware. Fix: a new `fetch_path_max_change_class(url, from, to)` pulls the compat doc at `to`'s tarball, walks records in the `(from, to]` half-open range, returns the strictest class via the `path_max_change_class` pure helper. The reconcile destructive check replaces `fetch_change_class(url, target)` → `fetch_path_max_change_class(url, current_target, desired)`. Edge cases: from==to → Safe (no-op); from>to (downgrade) → Safe (spec.md is silent on the downgrade direction; conservative default; future work for cumulative reverse-direction semantics if a real use case surfaces); unparseable version key → skipped without affecting other entries. Classification ordering (Safe < RequiresRestart < DataMigration < Breaking) via an internal `class_order` helper. +8 regression unit tests (path-max strictest-in-range, excludes-from, no-op, downgrade, requires-restart > safe, data-migration > requires-restart, breaking > data-migration, skip unparseable). Total platform-stack crate: 68 → 76. CLI 0.1.133 → 0.1.134, operator+webhook chart v0.1.114 → v0.1.115 + appVersion v0.1.133 → v0.1.134, platform-stack 0.1.37 → 0.1.38 | v0.1.134 |
| 2026-05-23 | M1.5 Walk-fix #7 post-B.1.78 — `PlatformMigrationStrategy.reject` failed for channel-following clusters (snapshot.pin=null). The Phase B.1.78 reject test walk on chart 0.1.36 (breaking) created plan `platform-0-1-35-to-0-1-36`, the user patched it to phase=rejected. The MigrationController saw rejected → invoked strategy.reject → built an SSA-apply body `{"spec":{"pin":null}}` (snapshot.pin=null since the cluster is channel-following). Apiserver 422: `spec.pin: Invalid value: "null": spec.pin in body must be of type string` (CRD schema `type: string` without `nullable: true`). The error propagated → reconcile errored → walk-fix #3 sealing's `status.rejectedAt` write never ran → the marker stayed null → the next reconcile retried the same path → infinite loop. Cluster blocking-by-rejected-plan still worked (plan phase=rejected set by the user patch; PlatformController GET-by-name found a non-completed phase → blocked the bump), but there was error log churn + broken sealing. Fix: three-branch dispatch in `PlatformMigrationStrategy.reject` — (1) Api::get the current PlatformStack; (2) a `pins_equal` helper compares the current spec.pin vs snapshot.pin treating missing/null/explicit-null as equivalent ("channel-following"); (3) dispatch: pins equal → no-op success; snapshot=Some(String) and they differ → SSA force=true (existing path); snapshot=None/Null and pin set → JSON merge-patch `{"spec":{"pin":null}}` (RFC 7396: null deletes the field, works regardless of CRD nullable). Side-effect: the walk-fix #3 sealing path now reaches completion for null-snapshot clusters (it was masked by this bug); subsequent reconciles see the rejectedAt marker and skip strategy.reject. +4 regression tests (pins_equal: missing/null/explicit-null equivalence + same string equal + different string distinct + null vs string distinct). Total migration crate: 14 → 18. CLI 0.1.132 → 0.1.133, operator+webhook chart v0.1.113 → v0.1.114 + appVersion v0.1.132 → v0.1.133, platform-stack 0.1.36 → 0.1.37 | v0.1.133 |
| 2026-05-23 | M1.5 Track B.1.78 closure — PlatformController MigrationPlan integration per spec.md §3.11 + ADR 0027. The PlatformController reconcile body gains a destructive-transition gate: it synthesizes a deterministic plan name `platform-<from>-to-<to>` (dots → dashes for DNS-1123), GETs by name in `apprafter-system`, then runs a 4-branch flow: (1) plan exists + phase=completed → bump (operator approved + ran the migration); (2) plan exists + other phase (pending/approved/executing/failed/**rejected** — rejected blocks too per ADR 0027 explicit decision) → block the bump, surface MigrationPending=True/<class from existing plan> + UpgradeAvailable=True/BlockedByMigrationPlan with `apprafter-system/<plan-name>` in the message; (3) no plan + classification destructive (`breaking|data-migration|requires-restart` per spec.md §3.11 — extended beyond the prior code's `breaking|data-migration` only) → CREATE a MigrationPlan via SSA with scope.type=platform, scope.platform.components=["platform-stack"] (a simplification — single conservative entry; future enhancement: diff per-component), trigger.{type=platform-classification, field=spec.pin, from, to}, risks.classification, **previousSpecSnapshot.pin** = current spec.pin (or JSON null when unpinned — `PlatformMigrationStrategy.reject` B.1.76 reads it back for revert), block the bump, surface conditions; (4) no plan + safe → bump as before. **The annotation source per the plan.md placeholder was rewritten to a structured `spec.previousSpecSnapshot.pin` field** (already in the B.1.75 CRD). Approve flow: the MigrationController completes the plan → the PlatformController's next reconcile sees completed → bumps. Reject flow: B.1.76's PlatformMigrationStrategy.reject reverts the pin. | 2026-05-23 | M1.5 Walk-fix #6 post-B.1.77 — the webhook config was missing the `migrationplans/status` rule. Phase 3.4 retest walk on v0.1.130: we applied an app-scope MigrationPlan, patched it with `--subresource=status --type=merge -p '{"status":{"phase":"rejected"}}'`. The webhook should have denied it per ADR 0027 (walk-fix #2 validator guard), but the patch succeeded. Root cause: the ValidatingWebhookConfiguration's `migrationplans.apprafter.io` webhook listed only `resources: [migrationplans]`. `kubectl patch --subresource=status` routes through the apiserver's `/status` SUB-resource endpoint — a separate path from the main resource. Webhook configs must explicitly list `<resource>/status` to intercept status-subresource writes; without it, status patches bypass the webhook entirely → the walk-fix #2 ADR 0027 guard and the phase transition FSM are never invoked for status changes. Fix: chart `operator/charts/apprafter-admission-webhook/templates/validatingwebhookconfiguration.yaml` — add `migrationplans/status` to the migrationplans webhook's `rules.resources` list. No Rust code change; the operator + webhook binaries are identical to v0.1.130 (image v0.1.131 tagged via standard chart lockstep, same binary content). **Bonus:** chart 0.1.33 pins the identical image v0.1.131 as the v0.1.130 cluster runs after chart 0.1.32 — pinning to 0.1.33 triggers no pod restart, enabling clean isolated walk-fix #5 verification on a stable pod without chart-upgrade pod-cycle artifacts (which caused the Phase 6 second-bump regression — the bump landed on an intermediate v0.1.127 pod without walk-fix #5, the strip pattern wiped the entry). | 2026-05-23 | M1.5 Walk-fix #5 post-B.1.77 — versionHistory SSA ownership-release bug. The walk-fix #4 observability in v0.1.129 showed that settled-state reconciles always log `include_version_history=false new_history_len=0` — the strip pattern is active every cycle. Combined with the CRD schema verified (versionHistory present) + apiserver pruning ruled out + the Kubernetes SSA Apply docs being explicit: "If a field is no longer in the applied configuration, the manager's ownership is removed; if no other manager owns it, **the apiserver removes the field**" — this is clearly the root cause of walk-fix #7's "omit field to preserve value" pattern. Sequence: the bump cycle's append + write claims ownership + stores the entry; immediately the next settled-state reconcile strips the field + releases ownership → the apiserver deletes it within ~30s. All earlier walks saw null versionHistory because the reads happened AFTER ownership release. **Fix:** drop the "omit field" pattern; replace it with a server-state-read + merge pattern. Before each `patch_status`, `Api::get_status` reads the authoritative server state; the `merge_version_history(server, local)` helper (new in `status.rs`) preserves server entries, appends local-only ones, dedupes by `(version, appliedAt)` pair, enforces the cap. The patch body ALWAYS includes `versionHistory`. Cost: an extra `Api::get_status` per write; settled cycles skip via the `write_status_if_changed` shortcut (no-op writes early-return). It sees through the cache-stale-overwrite race that walk-fix #7 was originally protecting against — it never reads the cache for versionHistory, always reads the server. The `_include_version_history` parameter is retained as a no-op for binary-compat with existing call sites. +4 regression tests in `status.rs`: `merge_version_history_keeps_server_entries_when_local_is_empty` (load-bearing — settled state preserves server entries), `_appends_local_only_entries` (bump cycle), `_dedupes_by_version_and_applied_at` (rollback semantics), `_caps_at_max` (ring buffer). | 2026-05-23 | M1.5 Walk-fix #3 + #4 post-B.1.77 bundled. **#3:** the MigrationController seals rejected plans via a persistent `status.rejectedAt` marker. The acceptance walk showed that the platform-scope plan walk-platform-1 (rejected, previousSpecSnapshot.pin="0.1.25") forced PlatformStack.spec.pin="0.1.25" on every operator pod restart (the chart auto-upgrade triggered a Deployment rolling update → cold-start cache replay → rejected plans re-reconciled → strategy.reject() re-invoked → SSA-patched the pin back to the snapshot). This overrode the user's `kubectl patch ... pin=null` patches and jammed the PlatformController in a `fetch_change_class("0.1.25")` registry error loop (0.1.25 is not published). Fix: a persistent marker `status.rejectedAt: Option<String>` (RFC3339 timestamp). The reconcile's `"rejected"` branch checks the marker — if present, skip strategy.reject + `Action::await_change()`; if absent, call strategy.reject + set the marker + write status. The plan is sealed after the first reject; subsequent reconciles on cold-start are no-ops. The CRD schema (operator chart's `crd-migrationplan.yaml`) + CUE source (`schemas/v1alpha1/migrationplan.cue`) were extended with `status.rejectedAt: string format=date-time` (optional). The Rust type `operator_core::MigrationPlanStatus.rejected_at: Option<String>`. +2 regression tests: `rejected_plan_with_rejected_at_marker_is_sealed`, `rejected_plan_without_rejected_at_marker_is_not_sealed`. Total in the migration crate: 12 → 14. **#4:** PlatformController bump-cycle observability — two `info!()` logs around the append + write_status decision: (1) before append — `target_changed`, `appended_history`, `target_for_patch`, `current_target`, `prior_history_len`; (2) before write — `include_version_history`, `new_history_len`. The Phase 6 walk (artificial pin downgrade+upgrade test) showed versionHistory empty after multiple successful bumps; the logs showed nothing diagnostic (only generic "fired"/"completed"). These are production-useful logs (not debug); a future walk-fix may follow with the actual versionHistory write fix once the logs reveal the offending branch. | 2026-05-22 | M1.5 Walk-fix #2 post-B.1.77 — the webhook FSM closes the ADR 0027 bypass on app-scope `rejected` via first-write. The acceptance walk Phase 3.4 on v0.1.127: an app-scope plan applied + `kubectl patch --subresource=status -p '{"status":{"phase":"rejected"}}'` — the webhook **accepted** it (it must have denied it per ADR 0027). Root cause: the `is_allowed_phase_transition` first-write branch (`old_phase.is_empty()` — a fresh CR without status) returned `true` for any plausible new_phase **regardless of scope**; the scope check for rejected was only in the `("pending-approval", "rejected")` match arm later. So a fresh-CR + status patch slipped straight to a sealed `rejected` without tripping ADR 0027. Fix: the ADR 0027 guard moved BEFORE the first-write fast-path — `new_phase=="rejected" && scope_type=="application" → false` covers all paths (fresh → rejected, pending → rejected, approved → rejected defensive, executing → rejected defensive). The error message extended to "ADR 0027" for any new_phase=rejected on app-scope (was only the pending → rejected case). No code damage from the slip — `ApplicationMigrationStrategy.reject` is Ok-no-op per design; semantically it is just an audit-trail violation. +3 regression unit tests: `rejects_application_scope_first_write_to_rejected_per_adr_0027` (load-bearing), `allows_platform_scope_first_write_to_rejected` (counterpart pin for platform), `rejects_application_scope_approved_to_rejected_per_adr_0027` (defensive). | 2026-05-22 | M1.5 Walk-fix #1 post-B.1.76 — SSA `.force()` on MigrationController + PlatformController status writes. The acceptance walk B.1.74→B.1.77 on v0.1.126 hit a phase=approved freeze on a MigrationPlan: `kubectl patch ... --subresource=status --type=merge` registers the `kubectl-patch` field manager as owner of `status.phase`; the controller's SSA patch carrying `phase=executing` (under the `migration-controller` field manager) 409s with a managedFields conflict; error_policy retries every 15s forever on the same conflict. Symptom: the plan freezes at `approved`, no transition. The walk diagnosis confirmed the root cause; the workaround for the active walk = pass `--field-manager=migration-controller` on the kubectl patch (kubectl pretends to be the controller, no conflict — the controller's next write reuses the same manager, owns the field). The real fix: `.force()` on `PatchParams::apply(FIELD_MANAGER)` in `operator-controllers/migration::reconcile::write_status` + preventively `operator-controllers/platform-stack::reconcile::write_status` (latent — the PlatformController hasn't surfaced the bug since it was the sole writer in every walk so far, but the structural shape is identical). The Application controller's `apply_status` already uses `.force()` (built-in since the B.1.7 era) — the walk-fix brings migration + platform in line. **No** regression test is added — this is a runtime SSA conflict requiring a real apiserver; it is covered by the next walk's re-run of phase 3.2. | 2026-05-22 | M1.5 Track B.1.74 walk-fix #7 — versionHistory race fix. The B.1.74 acceptance walk on v0.1.121 revealed a controller-cache race: the PlatformController's own SSA-status-write triggers a follow-up reconcile via the watcher cache (kube-rs `Controller`), which lags the apiserver by hundreds of ms. The next reconcile sees a **stale** snapshot of `PlatformStack` (without the just-persisted versionHistory entry); although `target_changed=false` (no append happens), `write_status_if_changed` still detects a `conditions[*].reason` delta (fresh Synced cycle messaging) and patches status — clobbering the apiserver's authoritative versionHistory with the stale-2-entry-vector. Walk symptom: after the bump v0.1.19→v0.1.20 the history briefly shows 3 entries, then silently reverts to 2. Fix (Option A): the SSA patch body now OMITS the `versionHistory` field when the reconcile cycle did NOT append this turn. SSA preserves field values that are ABSENT from the patch body, so the apiserver's existing value stays authoritative across stale-cache reconciles. Implementation: three internal helpers (`build_status_patch`, `write_status`, `write_status_if_changed`) gained an `include_version_history: bool` parameter; `build_status_patch` serializes the status to JSON and conditionally strips the `versionHistory` map key. The reconcile body tracks an `appended_history` flag and passes it through. The in-flight early-return passes `false` (no append ever happens before that branch). Option B (read PlatformStack via `Api::get` bypassing the cache) was rejected — a per-reconcile apiserver round-trip on the hottest path + it doesn't actually close the race (`Api::get` is still subject to read-your-write timing). +2 regression tests: `build_status_patch_omits_version_history_when_not_appended` + `build_status_patch_includes_version_history_when_appended`; the existing `build_status_patch_includes_apiversion_kind_and_name` updated to the new 3-arg signature (`include_version_history: true` for the existing assertion). Total 48→50 in the platform-stack controller crate. Per the user, "Let's do A without a re-walk and go straight to 1.74a" — the walk re-verification is skipped, B.1.74a (yanking) is next. Entry: `{version, appliedAt, outcome: "succeeded"}`. (2) **`Ready` condition** — derived from `parent.status.health.status`. | 2026-05-22 | M1.5 Track B.1.73 walk-fix #6 — observability polish for foreign-writer detection. A steady-state cluster on v0.1.119 showed: `kubectl patch parent target=0.1.19` → the controller force-reverted it (visible in the Argo CD UI), but there was zero durable trace: the `UnauthorizedSourceModification=True` condition flipped back to `False/Clean` within the next reconcile (sub-second), and the WARN log was lost during the pod restart cascade (chart 0.1.19 has a different operator image pin → Argo CD triggered a pod replacement in the middle of the revert). Fix: emit two Kubernetes Events per violation through `kube::runtime::events::Recorder` — `Warning/ForeignFieldManager` at detection (naming the offending manager) + `Normal/SourceReverted` after a successful force-revert. Both target the PlatformStack singleton with the parent Argo CD Application as `secondary` (`related`). Best-effort publish (failures logged at warn!, do not fail the reconcile). The operator chart's ClusterRole gains an `events.k8s.io/events` create+patch rule (kube-rs `Recorder::publish` uses the events.k8s.io/v1 API, not the legacy core group). Visible via `kubectl describe platformstack default` + `kubectl get events -n apprafter-system`. Survives an operator pod restart (k8s event TTL default 1h). +1 regression test (`parent_object_reference_points_at_argocd_application`) pinning the shape of the `related` ObjectReference. Live verification deferred to the B.1.74 walk (will exercise event emission as a regression check during MigrationPlan tests). | 2026-05-22 | M1.5 Track B.1.73 walk-fix #5 — the fifth post-closure walk on v0.1.118: the Phase 1-3 acceptance passed cleanly (status correctly populated, conditions sensible, no false-positive `UnauthorizedSourceModification`, managedFields has platform-controller). But the logs showed **hundreds of reconciles per second** — `reconcile fired generation=2` + `reconcile completed` repeating ~350ms infinitely. Root cause: every reconcile unconditionally (1) queried OCI for channel-latest, (2) stamped `status.lastUpstreamCheck = Utc::now()`, (3) SSA-patched status with the new timestamp. The status patch → resource version bump → the watcher fires a fresh event → the next reconcile repeats. A tight self-feedback loop, CPU pegged. Also test 2 was broken: on `kubectl patch` parent target=0.1.15 the controller's force-revert race is lost in noise — Argo CD manages to pull chart 0.1.15, replace the ClusterRole with the old version (without platformstacks rules), the controller becomes locked out. Fix two-pronged: (1) **OCI poll throttle** `MIN_OCI_POLL_INTERVAL_SECS=60` — `reconcile()` reads the prior `status.lastUpstreamCheck`, re-queries OCI only when ≥60s have elapsed. Intermediate reconciles preserve the prior availableVersion + do not touch lastUpstreamCheck. (2) **`write_status_if_changed`** — a new wrapper compares `new_status` vs `stack.status` (PartialEq derived); byte-equal → skip the SSA patch. Combined with `condition()`'s transition-time preservation: a no-op reconcile produces an identical status, the patch never fires, no watch event, the loop dies. Steady-state behaviour is now ~0.017 Hz (1 reconcile/min) vs the 100+ Hz tight loop. +2 regression tests (`status_equality_treats_identical_payloads_as_noop`, `status_equality_distinguishes_timestamp_changes`); total 42→44. CLI 0.1.118 → 0.1.119, operator+webhook chart v0.1.99 → v0.1.100 + appVersion v0.1.118 → v0.1.119, platform-stack 0.1.20 → 0.1.21 | v0.1.119 |
| 2026-05-22 | M1.5 Track B.1.73 walk-fix #4 — the fourth post-closure walk on v0.1.117. The PlatformController logs finally showed the real failure: `parse compatibility.yaml: missing field "compatibility"` every reconcile cycle. The chart's render_tool.cue emits compatibility.yaml as a **top-level map keyed by version** (`"0.1.19":` directly), but the parser expected an outer wrapper `compatibility:`. It also surfaced 4 related problems — a combined 5-fix walk-fix: (1) **Parser fix**: `type CompatibilityDoc = BTreeMap<String, VersionRecord>` — a direct map. (2) **Observability**: info!() logs at every point (spawn, watch loop entry, reconcile fire/finish, SSA patch, foreign detection). Without them walk-fix #1 (RBAC) was invisible — the controller was failing silently. (3) **Loader SSA + whitelist `apprafter-cli`**: step 3 (root App) switched from client-side `kubectl apply` to `apply_manifest_server_side` field manager `apprafter-cli`; the controller's `WHITELISTED_FIELD_MANAGERS` whitelists this manager. It closes the false-positive `UnauthorizedSourceModification=True / ForeignFieldManager: kubectl-client-side-apply` which triggered itself on every bootstrap. (4) **The controller watches the parent Application** via `Controller::watches_with()` — manual kubectl-patches on the parent App now fire an immediate reconcile (previously — wait 6h checkInterval). (5) **Single-writer SSA pattern**: `patch_application` is always `force=true`. The old "patch without force, then force-revert if foreign" deadlocked when the loader's kubectl-client-side-apply already owned targetRevision — SSA without force 409'd, reconcile failed before the revert path. The PlatformController is now THE single writer for `spec.source.{targetRevision, helm.valuesObject}`; foreign detection is only an audit condition + warn log. Known limitation **documented** (not engineered): a manual kubectl-patch of parent target to a chart version pre-0.1.17 (without PlatformStack RBAC) breaks the PlatformController because Argo CD overwrites the ClusterRole with the old version. Recovery: kubectl-patch back to 0.1.17+. An accepted degenerate case per the user (downgrade below the PlatformController-aware era). Tests 40→42 (+2 compatibility parser shape tests). | 2026-05-21 | M1.5 Track B.1.73 walk-fix #3 — the third post-closure walk on v0.1.116 (with RBAC + TypeMeta from the previous fixes): the PlatformController finally reconciled the CR and populated status, but surfaced two semantic bugs: (1) `UpgradeAvailable=True` fired even when `currentVersion == availableVersion == 0.1.18` — root cause: the old gate `current_target != desired_target ∨ values_differ()` falsely triggered on values_differ because the loader did not set `helm.valuesObject` on the parent App (null vs `{tier: 1}` looks like a diff); (2) `kubectl get app platform -n argocd -o jsonpath='{.metadata.managedFields[*].manager}'` showed only `kubectl-client-side-apply argocd-application-controller` — `platform-controller` made not a single patch because the policy (pin=None + autoUpgrade=false) refused. Fix end-to-end refactor of `reconcile()`: (a) a new `semver_gt(a, b)` helper, `UpgradeAvailable` is now a STRICT semver comparison `channel_latest > target_for_patch` independent of values diffs (fail-safe — false on unparseable); (b) the SSA patch ALWAYS owns both `targetRevision` (kept at current when policy forbids a bump) AND `helm.valuesObject` (values — runtime config, not a version bump, not gated by pin/autoUpgrade); (c) a new `platform_controller_owns_source(parent)` helper triggers a no-op SSA patch on the first reconcile when the manager is not yet registered — so that outside-writer detection works. Plus `MigrationPending` now has an explicit False/Clean; `Synced.reason` = `Patched` or `Reconciled` depending on the patch. +8 regression tests (5 `semver_gt_*` + 3 `platform_controller_owns_source_*`), total 32 → 40. CLI 0.1.116 → 0.1.117, operator+webhook chart v0.1.97 → v0.1.98 + appVersion v0.1.116 → v0.1.117, platform-stack 0.1.18 → 0.1.19 | v0.1.117 |
| 2026-05-21 | M1.5 Track B.1.73 walk-fix #2 — the second post-closure walk on v0.1.115 (with the RBAC fix): the operator pod starts correctly with the new RBAC, the PlatformController watcher works (the CR appears in the reconcile loop), but **every** reconcile fails with `ApiError: invalid object type: /, Kind=: BadRequest (400)` every minute. Root cause: `write_status` sent an SSA patch `{"status": {...}}` without TypeMeta (apiVersion + kind + metadata.name) — the apiserver recommends a full TypeMeta in the SSA patch body to resolve the schema before merge. The Application controller's `apply_status` always emitted TypeMeta correctly, but in the new B.1.73 `write_status` it was dropped. Fix: extracted `build_status_patch(name, status)` + `build_application_patch(desired)` helpers — both emit a full SSA-compliant body (apiVersion+kind+metadata.name+resource-specific). Two regression tests (`build_status_patch_includes_apiversion_kind_and_name`, `build_application_patch_includes_apiversion_kind_name_and_source`) pin the contract — future refactors will not be able to silently strip TypeMeta. Root cause: the operator chart's ClusterRole (B.1.71-era) granted permissions only on `apprafter.io/applications` + owned children. The PlatformController's watcher on `apprafter.io/platformstacks` failed silently on the first list/watch (Forbidden), the controller stream closed without emitting reconciles. The chart also lacked `argoproj.io/applications` get/patch — even if the watcher had worked, an SSA patch of the parent `platform` Application would be rejected. Fix: the chart's ClusterRole gains two rule blocks (platformstacks + /status + argoproj.io/applications). **Parallel optimizations**: both Deployments (operator + admission-webhook) dropped the 5-10s `initialDelaySeconds` padding on the liveness/readiness probes (Rust musl-static boots in ms); a `startupProbe` was added (1s period × 30s failureThreshold) for cold-boot grace without overhead on every restart. The operator workspace `[profile.release]` += `panic = "abort"` — removes the unwinding machinery, ~5-10% smaller musl-static image, faster pull. Net cold-start saving: ~10-20s on each of the two pods + a smaller image. Regression tests updated: `waits.len() == 8`, plus assertions that every `--for=create` precedes its matching `--for=condition=Established`. Open question deferred (why step 4b root Healthy false-positives — Argo CD's child-health aggregation timing): step 4c is now non-load-bearing, making root Healthy immaterial for the CRD readiness assertion. For each `needs` entry on the effective spec it SSA-applies one child `ResourceClaim` named `{app}-{type}` (DNS-1123-folded), default selector `{tier: integrated}` injected when the need omits one, `size` passed through when present, owner-ref → the Application (controller=true / blockOwnerDeletion=true) for cascade — writing **spec + metadata only, NEVER status** (the 2.3 scheduler owns status.provider/Scheduled, the 2.4c provisioner owns status.ready/connectionSecretRef/Ready; a strict SSA split under the `apprafter-operator` field manager, the apply payload carries no `status` key). | 2026-07-15 | **CRD-YAML fix (NOT a subphase) — `imagePolicy.resolve: "off"` was unusable (ADR 0040 image auto-deploy opt-out).** The generated `Application` CRD emitted the `spec.base.imagePolicy.resolve` enum (`"digest" \| "off"`) with **`off` as a BARE YAML scalar**; the apiserver parses CRD YAML with `sigs.k8s.io/yaml` (YAML 1.1), which coerces unquoted `off` → boolean `false`, so the compiled OpenAPI enum became `["digest", false]` and **hard-rejected** the string `"off"` the operator emits (`ImagePolicy.resolve: Some("off")`) — live proof `Unsupported value: "off": supported values: "digest", "false"`. The documented per-app opt-out of tag→digest auto-resolution was therefore completely unusable. Root cause is purely CRD-YAML serialization in **crdgen** (the CUE→CRD generator, ADR 0047): `serde_yaml` (a YAML 1.2 emitter) quotes the 1.2 keywords (`true`/`false`/`null`/`~`) but leaves the YAML-1.1-only boolean family (`y/n/yes/no/on/off` + case spellings) bare. Fix: crdgen now walks the typed CRD `Value` tree and force-quotes every YAML-1.1-ambiguous **string** enum value (leaving genuine booleans/numbers bare — the tree walk is what makes this correct, vs a text pass that would wrongly quote `served: true`), so the CRD ships `- 'off'` in both `base` + `environments` locations. **CUE unchanged** (`"digest" \| "off"` is correct), **Rust `ImagePolicy` unchanged** (already `Option<String>`), **cue-cmp unchanged** (no `schemas/v1alpha1` edit). Regression-guarded by a crdgen unit test + a `just crd-validate` server-side apply of `resolve: "off"` against a real kind apiserver (previously rejected, now accepted). No other YAML-ambiguous enum found across the 8 CRDs. | operator+admission-webhook charts v0.2.31→v0.2.32 (CRD template only; images roll in lockstep per the release-operator convention, reconciler functionally unchanged), platform-stack currentVersion 0.2.38→0.2.39 (compatibility change=safe, operatorVersion v0.2.32); NO cli/cue-cmp change → NO monorepo tag; operator/platform-stack v* workflow-made on push |

New CLI verbs **`apprafter backup enable/disable/status/prune/check/unlock`** — enable/disable/status are declarative `spec.backup` merge-patches after a fail-closed preflight (restic ≥0.14, the sealed credential Secret exists, repo reachability, an explicit `--i-have-saved-credentials` DR confirm); prune/check/unlock run **operator-side** with the operator's full S3 creds (`--credential-file` dotenv or env), never from the cluster; `prune` is host+format-aware and stamps `apprafter.io/last-prune` — plus `--staging-mode monolithic\|sequential` on local-pull `backup` and `restore s3:… --credential-file` (operator-creds contract, format auto-detected via manifest version). **StagingMode**: monolithic (default, 1 snapshot/run, manifest v1) / sequential (per-claim snapshot-set, manifest snapshot LAST as the commit-point, peak-disk = max(claim)). **Retention**: fixed `--host apprafter-backup` for stable restic grouping + a pure `plan_prune` (keep 7/4/6 over manifest representatives, forget by explicit id — NOT S3 lifecycle rules, which corrupt content-addressed packs). **Two-tier scoped creds**: operator owns full creds outside the cluster; the in-cluster Secret (default `enforce:operator`) scoped to Put/Get/List + Delete only on `locks/*` (append-only history — protects integrity/availability, NOT confidentiality, which rests on the off-cluster passphrase). Creds NEVER in chart values (only `credentialRef.name` via `envFrom:secretRef`); runner self-reports into a non-chart-owned `apprafter-backup-status` ConfigMap. Delivered via subagent-driven-development (6 chunks: backup-core factor → runner bin+image → schema → chart component → CLI verbs → close). ADR 0050 amended; `operator-guide/backup-restore.md` extended. Walks: `e2e/backup-s3-hetzner.sh` (real-Hetzner monolithic) + `e2e/backup-s3-sequential-kind.sh` (kind+MinIO sequential). **Gates green** (cli fmt/clippy -D/test --workspace + `just platform-stack-check` + strict mkdocs); the local-pull **file** backup + full-DR restore path was regression-validated GREEN on real Hetzner post-refactor (`restore-reprovision-hetzner.sh`); **⏳ real-Hetzner S3 walk-run is user-gated** (needs the runner image published + a Hetzner Object Storage bucket). **Release-coordination fix after the first push:** operatorVersion was initially left at the already-published v0.2.32 (2.4h imagePolicy fix) → `release-operator`'s two-axis gate skipped the rebuild → the operator side (spec.backup CRD field + projection) never shipped and ps 0.2.40 pinned the stale operator (backup silently non-functional); AND the cue-cmp drift guard failed (bundled `schemas/v1alpha1` changed without a version bump). | 2026-08-10 | **2.16e vertical autoscaling via VPA — CLOSED (live walk GREEN on 0.2.52)** (order-3.7, SR:A; ADR **0054** + spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-08-2.16e-vpa-design.md` (4 external review rounds) + plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-08-2.16e-vpa-autoscaling.md`). The operator right-sizes app requests **in-place** via upstream VPA: it emits **one `VerticalPodAutoscaler` per managed app-env** (`updateMode: InPlace` — NOT `InPlaceOrRecreate`, which evicts a single-replica pod on an infeasible resize; `containerName: "*"`; `controlledValues: RequestsOnly`; `minReplicas: 1` overriding the chart's `--min-replicas=2`; `minAllowed`=the 2.16d seed 32Mi/25m; `maxAllowed.memory`=the 512Mi seed limit), **prunes** it on the managed→pro transition (`pro_mode(effective)` flips true), and **mirrors** `status.recommendation` (target + uncappedTarget) into `Application.status.recommendedResources` in the single status payload. A three-valued cluster knob **`PlatformStack.spec.resources.autoscale.mode ∈ {full(default), up-only, off}`** (read-with-fallback, operator never writes spec) selects the update policy; `apprafter platform autoscale set|show` flips it (merge-patch, `set off` warns about the seed-revert asymmetry), `apprafter app status` shows the recommendation. Adds the **`vpa` component** (upstream `vertical-pod-autoscaler` 0.11.0 / VPA 1.7.1; self-managed `certGen` cert — no cert-manager; `failurePolicy: Ignore`; recommender tuned 24h×14/168h-half-life + `--memory-saver`; `--feature-gates=InPlaceOrRecreate=true` alpha gate; syncWave -4) + a `vpa_available` startup probe gating emit/apply/prune/read + `autoscaling.k8s.io` RBAC. **Memory corrects only within `[32Mi, 512Mi]`** (RequestsOnly hard-caps the request at the tier-invariant seed limit) — surfaced via `uncappedTarget`. **Supersedes the 2.16d D2 budget-close** (apps request their real working set; post-saturation headroom re-measured in the walk). Gates green: cargo fmt/clippy -D/test --workspace (operator 139 + cli 454) + just crd-check + just crd-validate (8 CRDs Established) + just platform-stack-check (renders 0.2.49). **Live walk (real Hetzner) GREEN end-to-end** (all scenarios; both token projects verified 0). The walk earned its keep — it caught **3 bugs invisible to unit/CRD/review**, all diagnosed deterministically on a local kind harness + fixed in follow-up releases: (1) the **mirror never persisted** — `recommendedResources` declared as a bare `{...}` rendered `type:object` with no properties/preserve-unknown → the apiserver PRUNED the nested fields on every status SSA → fix = STRUCTURAL status schema (**op v0.2.40 / cue-cmp 0.1.21 / ps 0.2.51**); (2) the **`vpa_available` startup probe raced the VPA-CRD install** (observed the operator probing 0.06s BEFORE the CRD landed → VPA silently OFF for the operator's lifetime — high severity) → fix = LAZY re-probe (`Arc<AtomicBool>`, re-check while false, latch true) (**op v0.2.41 / ps 0.2.52**); (3) the VPA **certgen Job was BestEffort** → resources added (**ps 0.2.50→0.2.51**). Recommender measured ~12Mi idle → seed right-sized. 2.16e ≠ Phase closure (order-3.7 pulled-up; §6 untouched). | operator+admission-webhook charts v0.2.38→**v0.2.39** (per-app VPA emit/apply/prune + mirror + RBAC); cue-cmp v0.1.19→**0.1.20** (`schemas/v1alpha1/{platformstack,application}.cue` → drift guard requires the bump); platform-stack currentVersion 0.2.48→**0.2.49** (new `vpa` component + compatibility change=**requires-restart**, operatorVersion v0.2.39); **cli 0.2.38→0.2.39** (`platform autoscale` + `app status` recommendation → monorepo tag **v0.2.39**). **Walk-fix follow-ups (no further CLI change → no more monorepo tags):** op **v0.2.39→v0.2.40→v0.2.41** (structural status CRD + lazy vpa_available re-probe), cue-cmp **0.1.20→0.1.21** (structural `recommendedResources`), ps **0.2.49→0.2.50→0.2.51→0.2.52** (certgen resources + mirror-CRD pin + probe-fix pin; each requires-restart/safe). operator/platform-stack/argocd-cue-cmp v* workflow-made on push. |

a span resolves from the repo root, a link target page-relative, and a span that is a link's *text* is checked as the target; globs are suppressed at resolution and the pattern set is `*` `?` `{` `}` but **not** `[`/`]`, because three tracked files carry brackets in their names and treating those as patterns would suppress those references hardest when one moves; the verdict is the **leading token** of the `## Status` body, so the four partially-superseded ADRs are correctly not findings (a substring rule produced 11 false positives and no true one). **`adr-check-ignore`** — the same machinery as its two siblings (typed reasons, `since=` aged against real tag dates, void when unaged, unused-audit), keyed on the canonical `ADR NNNN` so one key covers every spelling. **A committed census** — `docs/measurements/docs-health.json` holds seven counts compared **by value**: six obligation counts may not fall (growth passes silently), `exemptions` is equality in both directions, `docsgen metrics` re-records, and a missing or unparseable census is exit **2**, never 1. **Four mechanisms were dropped or replaced, each on measurement:** (1) **staleness gating dropped** — the oldest corpus page is 104 days old so a 180-day rule cannot fire for months, a 90-day rule fires on the same four abstract pages forever, 24 of 33 pages share two last-touched dates and cross any threshold together, both clocks reset on non-events (a licence-header sweep, a formatter pass), `git log -1 -- <path>` does not follow renames and one corpus page has already been renamed, and of the last 100 commits touching `schemas/**` or `cli.rs` **three** touched an in-scope page — if it returns, its shape is a diff against a published tag under an explicit pathspec (what `scripts/check-operator-version-bump.sh` already does), not elapsed days; (2) **typed `verified-by` bindings deferred** — 17 of the 28 tracked `e2e/*.sh` are run by nothing automated, including `backup-restore-walk.sh` which a corpus page already cites as its verification, so requiring the field on the ~20 dev/operator pages would manufacture ~15 claims that read as verified and are not; (3) **a dedicated anchor checker found unnecessary** — `validation.links.anchors: warn` under `--strict` already fails the build on a dead anchor and all 28 in-scope anchored links resolve; (4) **three earlier-proposed ratchets replaced** — `blocks_executed` is unimplementable (`run=` accepts only `local`, which the gate reports as a finding, so it reads `0 >= 0` forever), `check_none` at 0 prohibits rather than ratchets (it bans the documented exemption channel), and a byte-compared census would go red the day after it was committed because three of the proposed fields are functions of `now`. **All three new checks find ZERO defects in the corpus today** — measured, not hoped: they are regression guards installed deliberately **before** 2.19i's ~15 guides, because a guard installed after the content merely ratifies whatever the content already got wrong. Limits recorded rather than papered over: the census counts cardinality and never specificity (a substituted obligation costs nothing; prose carrying no counted claim is invisible), and `code-path` cannot see crate-relative paths (24 of them in `docs/reference/environment.md`, all correct today, one `cli/` prefix from being the defect the check exists for). `docs/contributing/documentation-gate.md` brought current (four documented classes → ten, plus five about the exemption machinery) and one error on it corrected: a tagless checkout is exit **1** (`exemption-unaged`), not exit 2 as the page claimed. `FEATURE_TRACKER.md` gains its first documentation-system row, `🚧` — four of ten subphases, and the site is still unpublished. | docs-only |

Two mkdocs build hooks in `docs/hooks/`, registered under a `hooks:` key; **nothing they produce is committed** — all of it derives from committed content at build time, so it cannot drift from the pages it describes. **1.
