So `on_config` resolves the alias (catching `ClassNotFound` and naming the consequence), asserts it resolves to **this** lexer rather than to something else that claimed the name, and requires a sample to come back with six token families (comment/keyword/string/name/number/operator) through pygments' token hierarchy, so retokenising to a sibling subtype does not misfire while losing a construct does; both failure modes were **watched firing** before the commit. ADR R1 called for "a mandatory unit test" — what shipped runs on every build (preview, `just lint`, CI) instead of in a suite that has to be invoked. **The plan's measurement did not reproduce and was corrected, not adjusted:** it recorded 34 fences; it is 37 tracked / 31 on-site, and a count requiring the fence to open the line returns 33 (silently missing three inside blockquotes and one indented under a list item) — the build settles it independently, the site-wide token-carrying block count rose by exactly 31. **2. A `description` on every indexed page** — 31 hand-written pages gained one, where before exactly ONE had; of the **34** in-scope pages 33 now carry front matter with a description, the hold-out being the root `README.md`, which is not a site page and has no index entry or meta tag to fill. Read twice (the `llms.txt` entry and the HTML `meta` description); pages emitting their **own** meta description went 28 → **60**, the remaining 60 being the 59 ADRs plus the 404 falling back to `site_description`. Three of the first drafts were rewritten before landing because each asserted something its page did not support — the field's cost and its limit, since a description is prose and nothing checks it but review. **3. `llms.txt` / `llms-full.txt` / a markdown twin per page** — measured on the built site: **119** published pages, 59 under `adr/`; **60** indexed, **119** bundled, **119** twinned. The ADRs are **bundled and twinned but NOT indexed** (each describes the world as it was when ratified — the same reason the drift gate holds them out of scope — and they are half of everything the site publishes, so indexing them would bury the guides); a generated sentence in the index says so and names the count. Two shapes the design did not anticipate: an **`Other pages`** section, because two published NON-ADR pages are `not_in_nav` (`license.md` and `contributing/README.md`; the list's third entry `adr/*` covers pages the index excludes anyway) and a purely nav-driven index would have dropped them silently (with it the index is COMPLETE over published non-ADR pages, which is what let the guard be written as completeness rather than as a restated list of group names), and nested nav sections carried as a **label prefix** rather than flattened, because flattened outright the *Reference* group listed two different pages as "Overview". The hook **fails the build** when an indexed page has no `description` — the plan stated that as a contract and nothing enforced it. **4. A new pass in `scripts/docs-check.sh`** (its second, after the strict build) covering **both** hooks: index exists + names the licence, its links carry an http/https scheme AND sit under `site_url` (two properties — the second alone is vacuous, since `site_url` comes from the same config the hook read), every link resolves to a page the site contains, every published non-ADR page listed **and every entry carrying the description that page's front matter authored**, each bundle entry and each twin **IS** its page's committed markdown, every page declared `not_in_nav` was in fact published, and every `cue` fence in a page's COMMITTED SOURCE matched a rendered block on that page that read it **as CUE** (a `//` line back as a comment token, a `#Definition` as a definition). **Every expectation derived from THE OTHER SIDE** — page set from the built site, content from the committed source — never a thing against itself and never against its own length; nothing restates a page list, a page count or a language. That rule was arrived at by attack over three adversarial passes, and every hole found was one violation of it: the lexer's only detector living INSIDE the hook it guarded (delete its line from `hooks:` and the detector goes with the deliverable — as do a later hook overwriting the registration and `use_pygments: false`); a "links are absolute" test taking its prefix from the config that wrote the links; an index-entry pattern that did not require the `: description` suffix at all, so a writer dropping every authored description left the pass reporting OK; length floors that a truncate-and-pad writer passed and that could not tell two swapped twins apart; a fence set read from the TWINS, so a writer stripping fenced blocks took 28 of 31 fences out of scope silently; a fence check asserting only that SOME lexer took, which a hook installing `class CUE(YamlLexer)` passed with every block tokenised as YAML; a fence scanner blind to `~~~cue` and to pymdownx's own ```` ```{.cue} ```` spelling (while `cli/docsgen/src/scan.rs::fence_open` already accepted `~` — two scanners in one gate disagreeing about what a fence is); and `exclude_docs` able to unpublish any page outside `nav:` with NO ERROR and NO WARNING (adding `adr/0*.md` dropped all 58 numbered ADRs while `docs/adr/README.md` went on publishing its index with every link dead — mkdocs reports that at INFO, which `--strict` does not promote, and `mkdocs.yml`'s own comment already named the hazard). And with `site_url` removed the build stayed green and published an index in which every URL read `None`. **Two things deliberately NOT built, each on a stated measurement:** (1) **`since` in front matter DROPPED** — a per-page version claim nothing can check (no gate reads it, no build fails on it, no reader can tell a current one from one correct two releases ago), the same shape that failed for `verified-by` a subphase earlier; the contrast that decides it is in-repo, since the `since=` on an *exemption* stays because the gate ages it against the real tag date and voids it at 180 days — the distinction is not the field name but whether anything in the tree can call the claim wrong. `audience` survives the same test from the other side by being **derivable** (front matter → nav group → nearest ancestor directory's group → `general`), so it is optional rather than required. (2) **`<llm-only>` / `<llm-exclude>` DEFERRED** — **zero use sites** in the whole repository; the markdown-twin transform is their natural home and that pass now exists, so adding them later is a handful of lines against a REAL use site rather than a mechanism guessed at in advance (the same reasoning is recorded in the hook for `--8<--` includes, also zero use sites, which a twin currently carries rather than resolves). The branch then gained an **eighth counter, `cue_fences`** — the lexer check had a zero-check rather than a floor, and a twin writer stripping fenced blocks took it from 31 checked fences to 3 with every guard green. It is recorded AND enforced by `docsgen` (the tool that reads the corpus) rather than by the Python pass (which reads the site), because a counter one tool records and another enforces is a seam; the scopes differ deliberately, the census covering the in-scope corpus and the Python pass every published page including `docs/adr/`. Adding the field forced a re-record, so the committed floors rose to this branch's corpus in one go: pages 33 → 34, invocations 384 → 385, code paths 73 → 90, ADR references 66 → 68, `cue_fences` new at 17; `identifiers`, `cue_documents` and `exemptions` unchanged, and nothing fell. A second sweep found what the first had left: `codepath.rs`'s "Measured over the corpus" paragraph carried seven figures of which FIVE no longer re-derived (it now states the two properties that carry the argument — every link target resolves, and the only unresolved code spans are the globs — behind a `corpus_census` test that prints the shape and asserts both); `scan.rs`'s "`sh` is 165 today" had been wrong at three successive commits; `gate.rs` still said "9 of the 66 in-scope citations" at 68; ADR 0057's "all 28 in-scope anchored links resolve" was falsified by this branch's own new page (the property holds, the tally did not); and the `file.md:NN` pass had covered `cli/docsgen/src` but not `cli/docsgen/tests`, where nine citations still pointed at lines the front matter had moved. A **fourth** adversarial pass then found that the whole of pass 2 read the ARTEFACTS and never the published HTML — all three artefacts derive from `page.markdown`, which mkdocs settles BEFORE rendering, so an `on_page_content` hook stripping every `<p>` published a site with no prose and the gate stayed green reporting the twins, the bundle and the index all intact, because they were; deleting `admonition` from `markdown_extensions` (publishing `!!! warning "…"` as literal text) and an `on_post_page` hook stripping `meta name="description"` (removing half of this subphase's own feature) landed the same way. Pass 2 now reads each published page's `<article>` and requires it to carry that page's committed H1 and the longest runs of its committed prose, to have CONSUMED the lines the source wrote as block syntax, and to carry the page's authored description (or `site_description`) as its meta description. The same pass found three more of the one shape: `orphan_pages` asserted published ⊆ source and NOTHING asserted the reverse, so deleting a `nav:` line and adding the page to `exclude_docs` unpublished `operator-guide/node-prep.md` — the page ADR 0057 cites as the orphan nobody noticed for months — from the site, the index, the bundle and the twins with a clean `--strict` build (the missing direction is now asserted against the DRIFT GATE's scope rule, parsed out of `cli/docsgen/src/scan.rs`, so unpublishing a guide has to move a Rust constant too); `llms-full.txt` was compared by CARDINALITY, so dropping the largest operator guide and duplicating `license.md` left the count balanced and one page silently missing (a set comparison in both directions now, repeats named); and the index's SHAPE was unasserted with its label discarded, so reducing it to the licence line plus one flat `- [Page](url): description` per entry removed the H1, the site-description blockquote, every `##` section and the generated twin-rule sentence with the pass still printing OK (each is now derived from `mkdocs.yml`'s `site_name`/`site_description`/`copyright`/`nav`, from the literate-nav `SUMMARY.md` fragment, from each page's own `title:`/H1, and — for the twin-rule sentence — from `twin_for` and the count of published `adr/` pages; the guides-in / records-out split is asserted in both directions). Those seven are deliberate and each is a different thing: four absolute-URL specification citations; one `contributing/documentation-gate.md` naming `spec.md` as a REPOSITORY PATH in a table of paths the gate excludes (an https link there would make the page describe its own scope in terms it does not use); and two generated-reference link targets for the `apprafter plan` command page — a standing FALSE POSITIVE of the pattern, not a leak. Two stub sections retired** — `architecture/index.md` and `concepts/index.md` each opened with "**Status:** stub", pointed at a file the site does not carry, and held two of nine top-level nav slots, i.e. the first two things a visitor arriving from the landing page would click; deleted rather than resolved in place, because a page existing only to say it does not exist yet costs a click and returns nothing. **3. The other four "Operator" cells re-derived by finding each writer in the tree; each now names its command/component. **(4) `site_url` encoded the design decision 8 REJECTED** (`https://apprafter.dev/docs`, a path on the landing origin) and it is baked into every absolute URL in `llms.txt`/`llms-full.txt`/the twins/`sitemap.xml` — four artefact classes publishing the rejected design, so "the URLs are settled" was untrue while it stood. Now `https://docs.apprafter.dev/`, rebuilt, artefacts follow — and the PROOF first committed with it was itself false: "zero `apprafter.dev/docs` under the built site" re-derives as **4** (ADR 0057's rendered page, its twin, `llms-full.txt`, `search_index.json`), because the ADR's own prose describes the rejected origin; a raw-string grep cannot tell a GENERATED URL from a DISCUSSED one, so the assertion now reads only `sitemap.xml` + `llms.txt` and requires every absolute URL in them to be on the new origin. **(5) Three references to renamed pages survived OUTSIDE `docs/`** — a live link in `e2e/README.md`, a comment in `cli/docsgen/tests/invocation_test.rs`, and `e2e/lib.sh` printing a dead page name to an operator right before `exit 2`. That falsifies the rule the ADR institutionalised ("a rename is a `mkdocs --strict` problem, not a grep problem"): the gate's corpus is `git ls-files -- docs README.md`, `--strict` sees nothing outside `docs_dir`, `docs.yml` has no `e2e/**` trigger. Rule corrected, directories named, sweep command recorded. **(6) Both front doors failed their jobs** — the operator index linked 11 of 16 pages (backups mentioned twice, unlinked, inside lists of unbuilt futures, while `backup-restore.md` is a shipped 650-line guide) → rewritten as a job-grouped map with one "not documented yet" block and the false "per-tier guidance are live" dropped; the operator quickstart answered "run this on my own server" with `nix develop` + `cargo install --path cli/platform-cli` → now leads with the release binary; and `## Step 0 — run the k3d e2e first` opened four dependency guides, telling a site reader to run our own test suite as a mandatory gate → moved to the page foot as an optional contributor note. **(7) Minors:** `docs/index.md`'s net-new "generated reference for every command and OBJECT" (no generated object reference exists); `VERSION=v0.2.27` 17 releases stale on a copy-paste install line → replaced by a command that resolves the newest release; `docs/adr/README.md` rendering `# docs/adr/` as its browser-tab title and opening with contributor process → "Architecture Decision Records" + `description` + index under a lead; the "do not hand-edit `docs/reference/cli/`" instruction moved to `docs/contributing/documentation.md`; section indexes use page titles instead of bare filenames; `contributing/README.md` is links instead of a table of unlinked code spans; and the stale measurement in `cli/docsgen/src/health.rs` deleted for the property that re-derives. **(8) A FOURTH pass read the published pages and found four insider-vocabulary classes no pattern reaches** (the widened census reads 7 and all 7 are deliberate): "closure-blocking bug" in bold, four times on three dependency guides, inside the instruction a reader follows when data they were told was deleted is still there — rewritten to stop-and-report plus the consequence (a volume still billed, data still on disk, a credential that still opens the database); "SSA-split guard", a mandatory step that is really a regression assertion between two of the operator's OWN controllers with `SSA` never expanded — moved into each page's `For contributors` section, acronym expanded; the operator quickstart, rewritten in this same subphase to need no checkout, still requiring one TWICE (`kubectl apply -f manifests/tier-1/...` and a trailing `schemas/v1alpha1/`) — manifest now inlined as a heredoc the way `egress-policy.md` already did it, pointer now an absolute GitHub URL; and the documented fix for `apprafter::env::cue_not_found`, which any user of `apprafter app validate` / `app add` can hit, sending the reader to `nix develop` "from the repo root" — now leads with installing CUE 0.10+ or pointing `CUE_BIN` at a binary, notes the cluster validates server-side regardless, links the contributor page last. **(9) Three more ADR figures did not re-derive and one excluded corpus was undeclared**: `llms.txt` "59" / `llms-full.txt` "119" / `sitemap.xml` "117" re-derive as 60-on-59-lines / **118** / 117 and every one is a corpus total that rots (the `llms-full` one additionally self-referential) — deleted per P2, commands kept; the in-fence dominance table keeps its four ratios (they carry the argument, re-run unchanged) and drops the two figures whose instrument was never committed; and the census command EXCLUDES `docs/adr/` — 59 published pages behind a top-level nav tab, 57 matching the widened pattern — so the exclusion, its reason (an ADR is a dated record in the coordinates of its day; its index says exactly that; sweeping them would falsify the record) and its own re-derivation now sit in the same bullet as the command, scoping the claim to the guides and reference it covers. | 2026-08-19 | **2.19g documentation publication — CLOSED (seventh of ten subphases; track OPEN)** (branch `feat/2.19g-publication`; plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-19-docs-2.19g-publication.md`; **ADR 0057** carries a 2.19g amendment and its decision 8 stops being "decided, not yet implemented"; **no release, no monorepo tag** — and the versioning question is derived, not assumed: `git diff --name-only <base>..HEAD -- cli/ operator/ schemas/ platform-stack/ argocd-cue-cmp/` is EMPTY, so `apprafter --help` is byte-identical, the byte-compared `commands.json` did not move and no chart artefact changed; the convention is that `cli/Cargo.toml` rides a RELEASED tag in the commit that cuts it and docs/CI work cuts none; the new `ghcr.io/apprafter/docs` image is versioned by commit SHA rather than by a git tag, so publication introduces no tag stream of its own either). **THE SOFTWARE IS COMPLETE AND THE SITE IS NOT SERVING — that distinction is the entry.** Five manual steps stand between this branch and a reader, none of which the repository can take: the push, `apprafter target domain list` confirming the zone, a proxied Cloudflare record for `docs`, one `apprafter app add … --name docs --path docs-site --branch master`, and setting `docsUrl` in the CMS before landing the held-back landing commit; `docs/operator-guide/publish-the-docs-site.md` is the runbook for the middle three and is written to be followed without the ADR or the plan. **1. The site is built in the workflow under `nix develop`, never in the Dockerfile** — `docs-site/Dockerfile` is one stage over `caddy:2-alpine` copying in a PREBUILT `site/`, and the reason is in the file because it is the single most likely "improvement" a later contributor makes: a `pip install mkdocs-material` stage would be a second, unpinned toolchain producing the published site while the gate validates a differently-built one (ADR decision 2 introduced the one `python3.withPackages` environment against exactly this), and the drift would be invisible — both builds succeed, only the published bytes differ. **2. The release workflow gates ITSELF and does not lean on `docs.yml`** — workflows triggered by one event run independently, there is no `needs:` across them and nothing reads the other job's conclusion, so a push failing the gate would run this one to completion and publish; the path sets also diverge in the publishing direction (`docs-site/**` fires a release and is absent from `docs.yml`'s filter entirely); and `--strict` is no substitute — it misses a stale generated CLI reference, a claim that no longer resolves against the product, a broken `llms.txt`/twin, and a link into an `exclude_docs`'d page (mkdocs reports that at INFO, below `--strict`'s reach, and `scripts/docs-check.sh` greps it explicitly). So the release job runs `scripts/docs-check.sh`, shares `docs.yml`'s rust-cache key and checks out at `fetch-depth: 0` because `docsgen gate` ages exemptions against real tag dates. **3. The path filter fires on a change to the PUBLISHED BYTES and is NOT a copy of `exclude_docs`** (re-derive the exclusion list with `sed -n '/^exclude_docs:/,/^$/p' mkdocs.yml`): the internal changelog and `measurements/` are NEGATED — which is the ADR's own Re-evaluation bullet discharged, they touch `docs/` on nearly every commit and change nothing a reader sees; `superpowers/` is in neither list because the tree is gitignored and cannot appear in a push diff (a pattern for an unreachable case is dead config); and `hooks/` + `reference/cli/SUMMARY.md` are excluded from the SITE but deliberately FIRE — the hooks RUN at build time and shape every page, `SUMMARY.md` IS the nav of every published page, so a filter transliterated from `exclude_docs` would have made a hook rewrite and a nav change unreleasable SILENTLY. `mkdocs.yml` + `flake.nix`/`flake.lock` fire (a theme bump rewrites every page's HTML and changes the fingerprinted asset filenames while nothing under `docs/` moves); `docs-site/**` fires because the Caddyfile is baked into the image (without it a cache-header fix reaches production only when someone next edits a page), with `docs-site/apprafter/**` carved back out because Argo CD reads the manifest from the cluster's config source — it is not an input to the image and editing it must not mint a digest; product source is absent by design (it changes what the gate CHECKS, not what the site CONTAINS, and the regeneration that must follow touches `docs/` and fires this). **The `exclude_docs` ↔ filter coupling is stated and UNENFORCED** — removing an entry publishes a page whose edits no longer cut a release, and nothing checks it. **4. The cache rules come from what mkdocs actually emits, and the landing's shape does not transliterate.** MkDocs fingerprints the bundle, the search worker and two stylesheets; the favicon and the lunr language stemmers under `assets/` carry NO digest — `find site/assets -type f | grep -vE '\.[0-9a-f]{8}\.min\.'`. A blanket `header /_astro/* … immutable` is correct on the landing (Astro hashes every name it emits) and would pin mutable URLs for a year here, so a theme upgrade changing a stemmer would never reach a reader holding the old one. Three disjoint matchers instead — digest form → `immutable`, the two undigested subtrees → one day, everything else → 300s — and anything under `assets/` matching neither falls through with NO `Cache-Control` deliberately (under-caching is recoverable; a year-long pin on a mutable URL is not). The `{8}` length anchor is load-bearing, not decoration: a loose `[0-9a-f]+` also matches `lunr.da.min.js` and `lunr.de.min.js`. HTML is 300s rather than the landing's hour because `search/search_index.json` is not fingerprinted and must not outlive the pages it indexes; `.md` is forced to `text/plain` because the base image's `mailcap` supplies `text/markdown`, which browsers offer as a DOWNLOAD, defeating the twins' purpose — an input this repository neither pins nor tests. **5. A new subdomain needs NO new zone and NO new certificate — re-derived, not cited**, because every future subdomain gets the same treatment: `platform-stack/cue/render_tool.cue` emits TWO HTTPS listeners per `gateway.allowedDomains` entry (`https-apex-<san>` and `https-wild-<san>`) both naming the same `importedCertRef` Secret, and the operator's `render_httproute` sets `parentRefs[0]` on the `platform` Gateway port 443 with NO `sectionName`, so a route attaches by hostname match and nothing has to be told the wildcard listener exists. The limit worth knowing: coverage is EXACTLY one label in both layers (`hostname_covered_by_zone` accepts the apex or a dot-free prefix; the CLI's `hostname_matches_domain` agrees), so a subdomain one level deep is free and anything deeper is a new zone. **6. The landing switch is a COMMIT OF ITS OWN and the ordering is in its message.** The tracked `landing/web/src/data/fallback/siteSettings.json` feeds the RELEASE path (the image builds with `LANDING_USE_FALLBACK=1`) while the Payload global feeds the PREVIEW path; `Header.astro` substitutes `<githubUrl>#readme` and badges the item "Soon" only while `docsUrl` is empty. The value is not typed from memory — byte-identical to `mkdocs.yml`'s `site_url`, host equal to `expose.hostname` in the manifest — and it was verified through the release path itself (`astro build` under `LANDING_USE_FALLBACK=1`, nav anchor read out of `dist/index.html` in BOTH states, so the check distinguishes rather than confirms itself). Held back because landing it before DNS resolves points the landing's front door at a host that does not answer — worse than the badge, because it looks shipped. **Findings reported and NOT fixed here:** (a) the landing serves **200** for unknown URLs — `try_files … /404.html` rewrites to a file that exists so `file_server` never fails, measured on the real `landing/web/Caddyfile` over the real built `dist`; the docs Caddyfile omits the fallback and returns a true 404, which is how the difference surfaced; (b) `scripts/lint-cue.sh` covers `schemas/`, `platform-stack/cue/` and `examples/` and NOT `docs-site/` or `landing/`, so both deployable manifests are validated by nobody — not hypothetical, `cue vet ./landing/web/apprafter` passes while `cue fmt --check` on the same directory FAILS naming both files, which is what an uncovered path looks like after a year; (c) SPDX enforcement reaches neither `docs-site/**` nor `landing/**` (no glob for either tree, no global `**/*.cue`), so the correct headers on the new files are unenforced; (d) `scripts/docs-artefacts-check.py` crashes with a `KeyError` on a NEW page that is written but not yet staged — `source` is built from `git ls-files` while `published` comes from the build output, and the `orphan_pages` guard that exists for exactly this case only appends to `problems`, BELOW the line that dereferences the missing key, so every contributor writing a new page meets a traceback before the guard's message. `FEATURE_TRACKER.md` stays **🚧** — seven of ten subphases, the site is still unserved, and the row flips `✅` at 2.19j. Census did not shrink on any counter (it grew with the runbook page); `just lint` green under `nix develop`. | docs-only |

What 2.19h contributed is the other half of that request plus the machinery that stops either half rotting. **Shipped:** a worked example on every one of the 75 visible leaf commands (124 lines) from one `pub const EXAMPLES` in `cli/platform-cli/src/examples.rs`, attached to `after_help` at run time in `run()` and read structurally by `docsgen` through the `docs_api` facade; `apprafter completion <shell>` over `clap_complete` for all five shells (the argument's type IS `clap_complete::Shell`, because `Shell` is `#[non_exhaustive]` and a hand-kept second list would freeze at today's five), with install recipes for bash/zsh/fish walked by hand under an isolated `HOME`; a `docsgen check` assertion resolving every example's command path and flag names against the clap tree; and two test files pinning that the block renders at all and that seven help properties do not rot. **The guard shipped BEFORE the examples, and the reason is structural:** `docsgen check` byte-compares the reference against a fresh render (proving the pages match the source, never that the source is correct) while `docsgen gate` excludes that tree because `check` owns it — so 124 invocations would have entered the byte-compared artefact unverified. Demonstrated with `docsgen generate` run FIRST, so the byte-compare was clean while `docs/reference/cli/secret.md` published `apprafter secret seal db-url --namesapce web`. **Examples are a structural table, not a delimiter in `after_help`, because a parse that finds nothing PASSES** — hence an unreadable entry is a finding, not a skip (five classes: bad path, bad flag, no `apprafter` invocation, empty, filed under the wrong command). Seven of the eight help-rot properties derive from the clap tree at test time (the eighth compares the shipped binary's help against the published projection — it is the standing check the dropped-`--version` repair needed, and its late arrival is why three documents said *seven* until a review re-counted the file); one of them rests on a COMMITTED list of the aliases the guides type, on purpose, because deriving that from the tree is exactly circular (delete an alias and a tree-derived list stops containing it, so the check passes on an alias that no longer exists) — and the seventh property is what keeps the list honest against the corpus. Derived, not assumed: `t`, `ls`, `info`, `rm`, `kc`, `cb`, `up` over seven distinct alias invocations on six instructional pages (dev-guide/quickstart + operator-guide/backup-restore, quickstart, shared-volumes, target-store, troubleshooting). **Two upstream `clap_complete` behaviours** recorded rather than worked around: a hidden subcommand still completes, and only bash/zsh complete a positional's value set. **FIVE items the (h) scope list named are NOT built and are named with a probe each rather than dropped:** `secret list`, a namespace picker for `secret seal`, `status.conditions[]` + remediation in `app status`, `requires = "select"` on `--namespace`, `app open --yes` — the first two have a trigger inside 2.19i, the last two are command-semantics changes needing their own decision.
